A City That Could Still Answer","volume":"7","anchor":"chapter-0080","paragraphs":["Cassian woke beneath a blue blanket with Mara asleep against his shoulder and Rowan sitting opposite them eating an apple. \"A beautiful scene,\" Rowan whispered. \"I have been waiting twenty minutes for somebody to appreciate it.\" Cassian looked down at Mara's hand curled around his sleeve. \"You could leave.\" \"And deprive you of an audience? It would be inconsiderate.\"","Mara opened one eye. \"If you mention our appearance, I shall put you back in the dairy van.\" Rowan raised both hands. \"I admire the firmness of your new administration.\" \"There is no new administration.\" \"Then the lack of a grievance procedure is deeply concerning.\" She threw a cushion at him. He caught it and finally carried his apple into the next room, leaving them the quiet he had pretended not to notice they needed.","The workshop belonged to an acquaintance of Mr Fenn. Its upstairs rooms had once been used to accommodate visiting actors, and the furniture retained an elaborate assortment of theatrical histories. A bust of a mournful monarch occupied the fireplace. The blanket came from an old production about pilgrims. Cassian looked at it. \"We have escaped the cow and entered the history department.\" Mara tucked the edge around him. \"Your cultural advancement is important to me.\"","He caught her hand and drew her down for a kiss. This one had no alarm behind it, no guard beyond a glass wall. The moment was almost cosy enough to hurt. She touched the bruising at his wrist. \"I hated that chair.\" \"I was not terribly attached to it either.\" She gave him a look. His smile softened. \"I am here.\" She rested her forehead against his. The fact could be said again without becoming less extraordinary.","Downstairs, the rescued clerk was speaking to a lawyer. The visiting engineer had contacted his family and planned to stay with a colleague. Both had agreed to describe what they had personally seen. The lawyer did not require them to become symbols of a movement before helping them find somewhere safe to sleep. The difference between assistance and recruitment mattered particularly to people who had spent the night under compulsory supervision.","Noor arrived after her morning rounds at Mercy House. Elena was safe and resting under the ward team's care; she had sent two messages, one asking whether Cassian could eat and the other informing Mara that she must. The ordinary concern made him close his eyes. \"Tell her I shall comply,\" he said. Noor checked his headache, examined the bruises, and told him to stop trying to disguise fatigue by standing very straight. His attempts at dignity contributed nothing to the examination.","The contact apparatus had not completed its pairing or begun a recent-memory operation. Cassian could account for the night and showed no new gap. That was a favorable finding, not a certificate of invulnerability. He would rest while the others prepared the next movement. Mara intended to enforce this instruction and was therefore disqualified from pretending she did not need sleep herself. Noor looked between them. \"I can manage one difficult patient. Do not make it a tournament.\"","By late morning, messages had begun to accumulate. Leah's labor group offered drivers and a repair crew. The department store workers supplied clothes and an array of inconspicuous bags. Dr Elin Varr brought an old observation instrument she had once used in teaching; it could help Ivo check the restored low-capacity line. Gideon sent a recording of his own account and said he was willing to let June use his name. He had made the decision himself, over Agnes's very audible objections to his tendency to dramatize everything.","Mara listened to the recording twice. The man who had once argued over porridge now spoke firmly about hours he could not remember and a company that had called him unreliable. He did not claim every patient had the same experience. He did not need to. His voice was one piece of a larger account, and it carried a particular weight because it sounded like a man speaking from a room he had finally chosen for himself.","Selene's owned media were already describing the annex rescue as an assault. A shortened clip showed Mara entering through the window and cut away before the contact equipment appeared. June obtained the longer recording from a staff member who had kept a copy. The omission had changed the narrative without changing the visible movements. They would release both with a clear explanation. A visual record could be accurate in what it included and misleading in what it left unmentioned.","The emergency channels would not carry that explanation. The independent observation link could carry voices, not giant files or an infinite supply of data. Ivo made the limitation very clear. They could use it to coordinate people at separate sites and direct listeners toward printed evidence. They could not feed the entire court archive through an old weather circuit and call the result a revolution. Rowan suggested that the limitation might improve the average length of political speeches.","The first broadcast would last only long enough to give practical information: where to find independent legal help, how to contact family-support volunteers, and which services remained open. It would not publish patient names or invite people to attack checkpoints. Mara would speak because people knew her voice. June would follow with the evidence. Leah would explain the delivery arrangements. Their organization needed a coordinator, not a monarch.","Mara stood before the small microphone and discovered that a room full of allies could be more frightening than a roof. Cassian, seated near the window, watched her without attempting to supply the words. He looked tired, deeply loved, and entirely certain that she could begin. She thought of the false inheritance she had lost. That costume had been borrowed. The woman standing here was not. She adjusted the microphone and looked at the first line of her notes.","\"My name is Mara Vale,\" she said. \"Some of you know me as a restorer. Some know that I have stolen things. All of you deserve a government that does not treat your memory as its property.\"","She did not use the broadcast to affirm her innocence of every earlier wrong. The court would deal with those questions, and she would answer for her own acts. The current emergency was not a reason to surrender that distinction. She named the help available, the documents that could be inspected, and the work already keeping the neighborhoods supplied. Then she asked people not to face a device or a guard alone if they could safely ask somebody to witness what happened.","Outside the workshop, a delivery truck stopped at the curb. Its driver listened to the small receiver on his dashboard. In a restaurant kitchen, a cook turned the volume up and told the staff to stop quarrelling over the pans. At the old academy, a classicist put down a book about the Revolutionary War and opened the door to a family needing directions. The message did not reach everybody. It reached enough people to begin moving from one conversation to another.","The official reply was immediate. A spokesperson announced that the unauthorized transmission had been brought completely under control. Ivo looked at his receiver, where Leah was still describing the food route. \"An ambitious adverb,\" he said. June nearly laughed into the microphone. The company had learned to announce a result before obtaining it. For a few precious minutes, people could hear the difference for themselves.","Then a warning came from the lookout. A patrol van had turned into the street. The workshop had become a place to leave. Mara folded the notes; June secured the recording; Rowan helped the rescued clerk into the rear vehicle. There was no time-consuming debate about whether they had accomplished enough to deserve an orderly exit. The observation equipment would travel in pieces and be put back together elsewhere. People came first, machinery after.","Cassian rose before Mara could object. \"Walking,\" he said. \"A medically modest activity.\" She gave him his coat. They went out through the back, crossed a narrow yard, and found the neighboring tailor holding open his shop door. He did not ask for an explanation. They moved between stacks of cloth while the patrol entered the workshop's public entrance. In the shop window stood a mannequin wearing a bridal veil. Rowan glanced at the couple. Mara pointed toward the exit before he could speak.","Their new safe room belonged to a printer whose premises had once been a pub. The surviving sign described it as a place of vibrant conversation, a claim that the dust did little to support. Rook found a kettle. Leah spread the tower plan on a pool table. Anika and Ivo inspected the maintenance list. They had obtained a route to one upper relay tier, but the main command panel remained under the company's control. The opportunity would come during the scheduled handover that night.","Peta sent a message through Noor. Several company clinical facilities still held trial subjects whose paired contact nodes remained in use. The network could not act on people merely because they had once encountered the particles. Elena and Ewan were disconnected and safe from that route of control. The remaining subjects were vulnerable because they were still being held at the stations. Getting them clear would require real people at real doors, not a clever announcement from a tower.","The work began to divide. The hospital and support teams would identify the remaining facilities and prepare safe transport. The engineers would inspect the tower route. June and Rowan would keep the documents moving beyond the emergency committee's reach. Mara watched the separate tasks find their owners and felt something steadier than optimism. There was still fear. There was also a way to use the next hour without pretending it contained the whole victory.","Cassian came to stand beside her when the room emptied. \"You did not call yourself a queen,\" he said. \"Would you have bowed?\" \"Only if you had asked very persuasively.\" She smiled, then slipped her hand beneath his coat and held him where the world could not quite take him away. Before the night was over there would be a tower, a company determined to keep its command, and people waiting at paired terminals. For this one moment there was a room full of maps, a kettle beginning to boil, and a man who had come back to her. She kissed him before the kettle could interrupt."],"questions":[{"type":"Scope / mechanism","stem":"According to Peta's message, which group remains vulnerable to the remote memory operation?","options":["Everyone who has ever encountered the particles, including disconnected patients.","Trial subjects still being held in contact with paired company nodes.","Everyone who hears the city broadcast, regardless of physical contact.","All users of ordinary medical monitors at Mercy House."],"answer":1,"explanation":"操作范围仍受配对节点与当下接触限制，颗粒暴露本身不构成远程读心条件。Elena与Ewan已经断开所以安全，Mercy普通监护器不是任意远控节点。","evidence":[21],"shuffled":true}],"wordCount":1755,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0080\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">7</div><h1>8. An Army of Aprons","volume":"7","anchor":"chapter-0081","paragraphs":["By seven, the alley behind the printer's shop had acquired an army of aprons. Some belonged to cooks, some to cleaners, and one spectacularly frilled example belonged to a traveling actor who had misunderstood the phrase \"unobtrusive clothing.\" Rook regarded it with the seriousness she usually reserved for poor weather. \"Can we use him as a signal flag?\" Rowan asked. The actor said his copyright terms were negotiable.","Mara stood in the doorway with the tower plan. They had a narrow maintenance window during the evening handover. The company had concentrated security at the front entrance, where its guests would watch Selene announce a new era of controllable public services. Contractors still had to move apparatus through the rear yard. Machines did not reorganize themselves because their owner had discovered the pleasures of command. The company needed people. Some of those people were in the alley.","Leah had found the crew whose names appeared on the authentic assignment. They were willing to collaborate, but two had families waiting in the district now being subjected to compulsory checks. The alliance moved those families to rooms offered by residents outside the immediate patrol route. It was not a recruitment reward. It was what had to happen before frightened workers could think about somebody else's problem. Mara helped carry their luggage and kept the map folded in her pocket until they were ready.","One man brought his daughter's school album. She would not leave it behind. Its pages contained a drawing of a bear, a photograph of a plum tree, and an essay written after a teacher's trip to Africa. The thinness of the book made its importance more painful. Mara put it in a dry bag rather than tell the child she needed only practical things. A life could not be measured by the weight of its useful equipment.","Back at the printer's, Tessa was helping compile the latest staff information. An omission in a contractor listing had become a valuable interval: the private security team was due to change at the same time the night maintenance crew arrived. It did not mean the tower would be undefended. It meant that people at two desks would briefly be uncertain which desk was supposed to answer a query. Mara had great faith in uncertainty when it wore a bureaucratic collar.","The team entering the tower would be small. Anika would accompany the actual maintenance crew. Ivo would handle the old observation link. Mara and Cassian would move the equipment and carry the copied plans. June would coordinate from outside, while Rowan and Leah kept the exit and transport routes active. Noor would stay at Mercy House, working with Peta to locate the remaining paired stations and prepare help for the subjects still confined there. Nobody would try to win every battle in one corridor.","Cassian's wrist was bruised but usable. Noor had made him demonstrate that he could grip without pretending pain was an interesting rumor. He passed the examination and received a look that promised no indulgence if he concealed a change. Mara watched with satisfaction. \"A savage regulatory authority,\" he told her afterward. \"You consistently require one.\" \"I had thought that was your vocation.\" She tightened his work glove and kissed his wrist where it was unmarked.","Their uniforms came from Fenn. His contribution to the defence of the city now included four coats, two caps, and an argument that a man's masculine pride was no reason to reject a properly fitted scarf. Cassian accepted the scarf. Ivo said he would rather take an exam in public speaking. Fenn looked at his clothes, sighed, and did something with a pin that made the engineer resemble a competent member of society. \"An unprecedented improvement,\" Rowan said.","Mara's coat had a reinforced inside pocket for the plans. She tucked her mother's silver flower beside them and left its chain under the cloth. The movement reminded her that she had put the key to her workshop there on the day the residents offered her a home. The key was still hers. Selene's occupation of a city channel could not change that small fact. It gave her something solid to carry alongside the fear.","Before they left, June put a recorder on the table. She wanted the team to state what they intended to accomplish, in case later accounts tried to turn a limited mission into a claim that they had taken over the whole system. They would preserve the relay logs, establish independent two-way communication, and prevent an emergency handover from locking out outside oversight. They would not deregulate every safeguard or shut down the ordinary city network. \"That last line is rather important,\" Ivo said. \"Some of us enjoy electricity.\"","The speaker in the public square announced that all unrest was under control. Around it, people were still disputing the checkpoints. The announcement's predictability had become a stimulus to doubt rather than obedience. A bank analyst who had supplied Rowan with records arrived carrying hot soup. He had never expected to be in the same camp as a thief and a port organizer. Over the years, apparently, his career had become more fulfilling in ways the executive suite would not approve.","The maintenance truck reached the tower at the back of a line of supply vehicles. From the window, Mara could see the upper tiers against the darkening sky. A giant screen showed Selene greeting a visiting delegation. The public hall below had been arranged like a theater, with a glossy backdrop and a rail to separate guests from staff. The stagecraft was excellent. Mara knew enough about stagecraft to be unimpressed by what it could conceal.","At the rear gate, the guard checked the crew's actual names against the assignment. Two real workers entered first, then Anika with the instrument case. Cassian wore a dust mask appropriate to the work area and kept his face turned toward the load. Mara handed over the equipment receipt. The guard had a complaint about the number of tin cases. The crew leader showed him the corresponding line. He counted again, found the same result, and allowed them through.","Inside, the tower sounded like a breathing machine. Fans hummed behind the walls. A monitor displayed the transfer sequence, while an in-house assistant repeated that the schedule could not change. The crew leader asked where to put a heavy frame that could not remain in the doorway. The assistant pointed upstairs without looking away from the screen. Mara took one end; Cassian took the other. They climbed slowly enough to look burdened and steadily enough not to be offered help.","On the fifth landing, they paused beside a cabinet. Ivo touched the wall map, compared it with the old drawing, and gave a small nod. The weather-service link was still physically separate at this level. Above it, the newer system crossed into the relay tier. They could not treat the whole tower as a single entity. Each route had its own apparatus, purpose, and risk. The map gave them a way to distinguish one from the other without guessing.","Anika joined them with the crew leader. She had found an unexpected inspection point at the upper lift. A temporary verification desk had been installed between the maintenance landing and the main relay room. It included a contact terminal. The assignment authorized entry, but a new order required selected workers to submit to verification before continuing. The phrasing was ambiguous about who had been selected. The guard's discretion was not a protection they could rely on.","Mara felt Cassian's shoulder go rigid beside her. She moved her hand until her knuckles brushed his. He did not need pity. He needed the information that she was still there and still thinking. They could withdraw down the stair, carrying the frame with them. They could also wait for the crew leader to ask exactly which part of the order governed his workers. The real staff had not agreed to have neurological apparatus introduced into an equipment delivery.","The crew leader took the question to the desk. He did not denounce the government or boast of his independence. He asked who would accept responsibility for delaying work if the order conflicted with the existing security procedure. The guard called his supervisor. For a moment, the plan held inside a conversation that could have occurred on any badly managed worksite. Then a second guard arrived with photographs.","Mara saw the top image before he reached the counter. It showed the depot. It showed her face beneath the courier's cap. She could not stand at the desk long enough to argue about identity. Anika's eyes moved toward a narrow service door. Mara shifted her grip on the frame. Cassian matched the movement before she spoke. They backed away from the queue as if the load was too wide, turned once, and slipped through the side door.","The room beyond contained cable drums, a maintenance ladder, and no easy way back into the lift lobby. Mara set down her end of the frame. The voices outside were growing sharper. A lock clicked in the door behind them. The company had finally noticed that its machinery required people who possessed opinions. It was taking steps to make those opinions easier to contain. Mara looked up the ladder toward the next tier. \"We have been given the scenic route,\" she whispered.","Cassian lifted the loose end of her scarf away from a protruding bolt. \"My favorite kind.\"","\"You said that about the dairy van.\"","\"I am adaptable.\"","Ivo was already examining the ladder. Above them, a maintenance hatch opened onto the older relay gallery. The route was awkward, cramped, and in need of precisely the sort of repair they had claimed to be bringing. It was also still a route. Mara reached for the first rail. They had not come this far to lose control of the plan because somebody else had closed one more door."],"questions":[{"type":"Inference / changing conditions","stem":"What does the team's experience at the upper lift suggest about its maintenance assignment?","options":["The crew has agreed to contact verification as a normal part of every repair.","Genuine authorization for the work does not guarantee passage through later, newly imposed checks.","The assignment was forged and had never authorized entry into the tower.","The assignment allows the team to disregard all safety procedures inside."],"answer":1,"explanation":"团队先凭真实工作人员名单进塔，但上层又临时增设核验点并出现照片，原工作许可不能自动解决新检查。不能说通行文件全是假，也不能把工作人员的授权扩大成同意所有检查。","evidence":[16,19],"shuffled":true}],"wordCount":1660,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0081\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">7</div><h1>9. Inference / changing conditions</p><p><strong>What does the team&#x27;s experience at the upper lift suggest about its maintenance assignment?</strong></p><div class=\"options\"><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"0\">A. The crew has agreed to contact verification as a normal part of every repair.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"1\">B. Genuine authorization for the work does not guarantee passage through later, newly imposed checks.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"2\">C. The assignment was forged and had never authorized entry into the tower.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"3\">D. The assignment allows the team to disregard all safety procedures inside.</button></div><div class=\"feedback\" hidden=\"hidden\"></div></div></section><nav class=\"chapter-nav\" aria-label=\"Chapter navigation\"><button data-go=\"79\">← Previous</button><button data-go=\"81\">Next →</button></nav></article>"},{"id":"v07c10","title":"10. The Hallway That Tried to Fold","volume":"7","anchor":"chapter-0082","paragraphs":["The ladder ended at a hatch that had been painted the same gray as the ceiling. Ivo called this an offensively modest piece of camouflage. Mara called it a door and opened it. Above lay a narrow gallery full of old weather instruments, a dismantled console, and the comparative luxury of air that did not smell of a cable cupboard. Cassian helped the real maintenance worker through behind them, then closed the hatch without allowing it to strike the frame.","They had lost the lift route. They had gained a position beside the old observation hub. The difference mattered chiefly to Ivo, who looked at the equipment with an appreciation that bordered on personal affection. \"Somebody kept this in good condition.\" \"Are you proposing?\" Mara asked. \"I am examining a robust connection. It is a desirable quality in a partnership.\" Cassian looked at her. \"I shall have to improve my wiring.\"","The engineer checked the plans against the console before touching the working lines. Anika joined them through a second service route with the maintenance crew leader. The company had ordered the crew to assemble downstairs, away from the upper tier. He had declined to leave a load halfway through a doorway without a written instruction. His argument had lasted long enough to give her a route past the desk. She now had the floor schedule and a very pronounced lack of patience.","A screen beside the console showed station reports. The entries corresponded to those Peta had described beneath the greenhouse: paired-node numbers, contact tests, recent-interval settings. No faces appeared. No line told them what a person was thinking. A contact state was not an inventory of a human mind. Mara copied the station identifiers while Cassian photographed the whole display, including the times and the status labels around them.","The screen's title described the report as a clinical coordination tool. Its last column was headed REMOTE START. Several entries remained enabled. Anika explained that this did not mean anybody hearing a radio could be affected. It meant that an operator could start a limited procedure at an already paired node where contact still existed. For those still held at such stations, the narrower description was frightening enough. They did not need to invent a larger menace to justify helping them.","Mara read the facility abbreviations aloud over the observation line. At Mercy House, Noor and Peta compared them with the documents already collected. Some could be matched to addresses. Others still required checking. The exchange was slow, deliberate, and possible only because the old line was carrying a small amount of voice rather than the whole stolen archive. At the end, Noor repeated the confirmed entries back. The two sides agreed on what had actually been said.","Downstairs, Selene's reception was beginning. A band played jazz in the public hall. Guests were being ushered past a portrait in a halo of projected light. The corporate board had promised an exhibit of the city's new order. Its opening statement praised normalization, stability, and the elimination of unreliable human input. Rowan, listening outside through a receiver, said he could think of several shareholders who had built their entire careers on unreliable human input.","June had reached the public entrance as a correspondent whose accreditation had not yet been updated in the front-desk listing.
